<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We disprove the Modified Integer Round-Up Conjecture for bin packing by constructing instances with arbitrarily large additive gaps between the configuration-LP value and the integral optimum. We also prove that, for every fixed nonnegative integer <i>c</i>, distinguishing instances that fit in <i>B</i> bins from those requiring more than $`B+c`$ bins is NP-hard. Both results hold with rational item sizes greater than 1/6, so each bin contains at most five items.

- **claim-unbounded-configuration-gap** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-unbounded-gap)): For every integer $c\ge0$, there is a finite rational bin-packing instance $I$ and an integer $B$ such that every item size exceeds $1/6$ and $$\label{eq:main-gap} \operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP}}(I)=B, \qquad \operatorname{OPT}(I)>B+c.$$ The equality for the fractional optimum also holds for the configuration LP whose patterns are subsets of the individual item copies.
  - Explanation: A finite construction gives arbitrarily large additive gaps for both configuration relaxations. The structural obstruction does not assume a separation of complexity classes.
- **claim-fixed-additive-hardness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-additive-hardness)): For every fixed integer $c\ge0$, it is NP-hard to distinguish pairs $(I,B)$ such that $I$ packs into $B$ bins from pairs such that $I$ cannot be packed into $B+c$ bins. Here $B$ is an integer supplied with the instance, the item sizes are rational, and every item size is strictly greater than $1/6$.
  - Explanation: Every fixed additive allowance leaves a hard promise distinction, even with rational sizes bounded away from small items. The threshold is supplied with the instance.
- **claim-constant-additive-complexity-equivalence** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-complexity-equiv)): A deterministic polynomial-time algorithm that always uses at most $\operatorname{OPT}(I)+C$ bins for some absolute constant $C$ exists if and only if $\mathsf P=\mathsf{NP}$.
  - Explanation: A deterministic constant-additive approximation exists exactly when the stated complexity classes coincide. The reverse implication constructs an optimal packing through decision and search.
- **claim-competing-depth-quota-trees** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-competing-trees)): For every integer $d\ge1$, there are two finite rooted trees $T^+,T^-$, an integer $L\ge1$, and positive integer quotas $p_1,\ldots,p_L$ with the following properties.* 1. *All leaves of both trees have depth $L$. Each tree $T^\sigma$, $\sigma\in\{+,-\}$, has a marking $\mathcal M^\sigma$ such that $$\label{eq:tree-separate-marking} \begin{aligned} \|\mathcal M^\sigma\cap V_\ell(T^\sigma)\|&\le p_\ell &&(1\le\ell\le L),\\ \|\mathcal M^\sigma\cap\pi\|&\ge d &&\text{for every root-to-leaf path $\pi$ in $T^\sigma$}. \end{aligned}$$* 2. *For any markings $\mathcal A^+$ and $\mathcal A^-$ satisfying the combined bounds $$\label{eq:tree-combined-quota} \|\mathcal A^+\cap V_\ell(T^+)\|+ \|\mathcal A^-\cap V_\ell(T^-)\|\le p_\ell \qquad(1\le\ell\le L),$$ at least one of the two trees has a root-to-leaf path that is not hit by its marking.* *The trees, quotas, and separate markings are constructible by a finite deterministic procedure depending only on $d$.
  - Explanation: Each tree separately supports repeated path hitting, but shared depth inventories cannot hit every path in both trees. The deterministic construction uses colored paths and preserves depths when pruning.
- **claim-graph-to-packing-reduction** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-thm-reduction)): Fix an integer $c\ge0$ and a real constant $\rho>0$. There is a deterministic polynomial-time construction with the following property. Its input is an explicitly listed simple graph $G=(V,E)$, with $V=\{1,\ldots,n\}$ and $m=\|E\|\ge1$, and an integer $0\le k\le n$. Its output is an integer $B$ and an explicit list of $5B$ rational item sizes in $(1/6,1)$ such that:* 1. *if $G$ has a vertex cover of size at most $k$, the items pack into $B$ unit-capacity bins;* 2. *any packing into at most $B+c$ bins yields a vertex cover of size at most $k+\rho n$.* *The running-time and output-length bounds are in ordinary binary encoding; their constants may depend on $c$ and $\rho$.
  - Explanation: The reduction preserves a cover-based packing and extracts an approximately bounded cover from every packing within the additive allowance. Polynomiality applies with the allowance and extraction tolerance fixed.
- **claim-nested-geometry-and-independent-order** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-geometry)): The embedding has the following properties.* 1. *Every child cell lies inside its parent. Every nonroot cell lies in the first tenth of its root cell. Distinct depth-$\ell$ cells across the two trees have gaps at least $3\lambda_\ell$.* 2. *The numbers $b_r$ belong to $[1/2,9/16)$, and distinct job cells have gaps at least $g-\Delta$. All the test intervals of one label are pairwise disjoint.* 3. *For $1\le\ell\le L$, $\Delta\le\lambda_\ell/10$ and $\lambda_1+2\Delta<g$. An interval of length at most $\lambda_\ell+\Delta$ meets at most one depth-$\ell$ cell. If it contains a point of a leaf cell, that depth-$\ell$ cell is the leaf’s ancestor. An interval of length at most $\lambda_1+\Delta$ meets at most one job cell.* 4. *For $r=(e,j)$, the two independent order bounds are $$\label{eq:coordinate-order} H_{r'}-H_r\ge\theta_e+1\quad(r'>r),\qquad \theta_f-\theta_e\ge H_{e,R}+1\quad(f>e).$$
  - Explanation: Nested cells separate local coverage choices, while distinct coordinate scales independently rule out later row positions and later edge resources.
- **claim-scalar-score-encoding** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-encoding)): Every item size belongs to $(1/6,1)$, so every feasible bin has at most five items. For a five-item tuple, zero total primary score is equivalent to following Table <a href="#tab:patterns" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tab:patterns">1</a>. A feasible five-item tuple has nonpositive total primary score; if that score is zero, its total secondary score is nonpositive. If both totals are zero, feasibility is equivalent to $\sum_iw(i)\le0$. The complete item inventory satisfies $\sum_i p(i)=\sum_iq(i)=0$.
  - Explanation: Separated perturbation scales enforce the subclass equations before label scores and coordinates. Zero primary score characterizes table patterns; equal labels require the additional secondary argument.
- **claim-local-good-anchor-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-good-anchors)): In any packing of the constructed instance into at most $B+c$ bins, fewer than $K$ items lie outside five-item table tuples. At each vertex label $v$, at most $3K$ anchors lie outside good tuples. This is a joint bound on its $\mathrm{Z}$ and $\mathrm{Y}$ anchors, and therefore applies to any subset of that label’s anchors.
  - Explanation: Primary-score conservation bounds global departures from table patterns. Label-prefix conservation then bounds anchor defects at each vertex without accumulating a loss for every job position.
- **claim-completion-and-key-feasibility** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-good-feasibility)): A good tuple has zero primary and secondary totals. Its feasibility is therefore described exactly as follows.* 1. *For kind $\mathrm M$, with row baseline $b$, deadline $z$, and associated global and local items $U,D$, define $$\label{eq:completion-coordinate} h=b-\Delta\mathbf{1}\{\text{row is short}\}-\operatorname{len}(U)-\operatorname{len}(D).$$ The tuple is feasible if and only if $h\le z$, and $h\le b$. For any collection of such row–auxiliary assignments and any $a\in\mathbb R$, $$\label{eq:completion-prefix} \#\{h\le a\}=\#\{b\le a\}+\#\{h\le a<b\}.$$ The last term counts coverage by the half-open intervals $[h,b)$.* 2. *For kind $\mathrm G$, let its key have position $r=(e,j)$, its row have position $r'$, and its global item belong to edge $f$. Feasibility is equivalent to $$\label{eq:key-feasibility} b_{r'}-b_r+\beta_f-\beta_e +\Delta\bigl(\mathbf{1}\{\text{nonpermit}\} -\mathbf{1}\{\text{row is short}\}\bigr)\le0.$$ It implies both $r'\le r$ and $f\le e$. For an exact-position long row, the global item must be a permit of edge $e$ or an item of an earlier edge. At the exact position and edge, a short row admits either a permit or a nonpermit, whereas a long row admits only a permit.
  - Explanation: Good deadline tuples reduce to completion-before-deadline matching. Good key tuples obey independent position and edge orders, with a permit restriction for exact-position long rows.
- **claim-integral-cover-completeness** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-completeness)): If the input graph has a vertex cover of size at most $k$, the constructed items can be packed into $B$ bins.
  - Explanation: A cover chooses the funded side at each vertex. Local coverage permits sorted deadline matching, and the cover condition assigns every edge permit and nonpermit to feasible remaining rows.
- **claim-exact-fractional-clique-packing** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-fractional)): For the instance constructed from the complete graph on four vertices with $k=2$, using the parameters in Equation <a href="#eq:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:parameters">[eq:parameters]</a>, both configuration relaxations have value $B$: $$\operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP,ind}}=\operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP}}=B.$$
  - Explanation: Averaging the selected and unselected local states matches every inventory fractionally. Distributing weights over physical copies proves individual-copy feasibility, and projection proves type feasibility; cardinality supplies the matching dual bound.
- **claim-arbitrary-packing-cover-soundness** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-soundness)): If the instance of Theorem <a href="#thm:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:reduction">6</a> packs into at most $B+c$ bins, then its input graph has a vertex cover of size at most $k+\rho n$.
  - Explanation: Extraction applies to arbitrary feasible packings within the allowance, including bins outside the intended patterns. The threshold cover is certified by resource conservation and bounded by the global inventories.
- **claim-test-point-coverage** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-coverage)): For every label $v$ and every point $a$ in one of its half-open test intervals, $$\label{eq:sound-test-coverage} I_v(a)\ge d-6K.$$
  - Explanation: Deadline demand forces a completion-prefix surplus. Earlier keys consume enough earlier rows to bound the baseline prefix, leaving the claimed coverage at every test point.
- **claim-forced-funded-side** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-side-choice)): Every vertex $v$ satisfies $$\label{eq:sound-side-choice} x_v^+\ge d-6K\qquad\text{or}\qquad x_v^-\ge d-6K.$$
  - Explanation: If neither side has the required global allocation, local intervals would induce quota-respecting markings hitting both trees. The competing-tree obstruction rules out that possibility.
- **claim-nonexcluded-long-job-matches** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-job-matches)): Every nonexcluded position $r\in\mathcal R_v$ satisfies $$\label{eq:sound-long-matches} L_{v,r}\ge d-x_v^+-9K.$$
  - Explanation: Displaced current-key matches reduce earlier rows available to deadline tuples. Accounting for that deficit forces exact-position long matches without charging a separate loss at every earlier position.
- **claim-threshold-vertex-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-edge-cover)): The set $$C=\{v:x_v^+>d/8\}$$ is a vertex cover.
  - Explanation: An edge with both endpoint allocations below the threshold would require too many long matches. Current-edge permits and the globally bounded residual earlier-edge supply cannot satisfy that demand.
- **claim-extracted-cover-cardinality** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cover-size)): The set $C$ in Lemma <a href="#lem:edge-cover" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:edge-cover">17</a> satisfies $$\|C\|\le \frac{dk}{d-6K}+\frac{48Kn}{d} \le k+\frac{60Kn}{d} \le k+\rho n.$$
  - Explanation: Large plus allocations are bounded by the plus inventory. Charging the forced side at every vertex bounds the remaining plus mass, and the prescribed parameter choice controls the total cover excess.

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Explicit rational bin-packing instance** ([source](#sec:introduction)): An instance of bin packing is an explicitly listed collection of rational item sizes in $(0,1]$, encoded in binary.
- **Feasible bin** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A bin may contain any subcollection of total size at most one.
- **Integral optimum and item-copy size** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Write $\operatorname{OPT}(I)$ for the minimum number of bins needed to pack an instance $I$, and $a_i$ for the size of its $i$th item copy.
- **Numerical size types and multiplicities** ([source](#stmt-def-configuration-lp)): For the ordinary type formulation, let $\mathcal S$ be the set of distinct numerical sizes and let $n_\sigma$ be the multiplicity of $\sigma\in\mathcal S$.
- **Type configuration** ([source](#stmt-def-configuration-lp)): Its configurations are all integer vectors $$\mathcal C=\left\{(c_\sigma)_{\sigma\in\mathcal S} \in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^{\mathcal S}: \sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal S}\sigma c_\sigma\le1\right\}.$$
- **Gilmore–Gomory relaxation** ([source](#eq:type-configuration-lp)): The Gilmore–Gomory relaxation is $$\label{eq:type-configuration-lp} \operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP}}= \min\left\{\sum_{\mathbf c\in\mathcal C}z_{\mathbf c}: z_{\mathbf c}\ge0,\quad \sum_{\mathbf c\in\mathcal C}c_\sigma z_{\mathbf c} \ge n_\sigma\quad(\sigma\in\mathcal S)\right\}.$$
- **Physical item indices** ([source](#eq:individual-configuration-lp)): Give the input items distinct physical indices in a set $\mathcal I$, with size $a_i$ at index $i$, even when some sizes coincide.
- **Individual-copy configuration** ([source](#eq:individual-configuration-lp)): An individual-copy configuration is a subset $H\subseteq\mathcal I$ with $\sum_{i\in H}a_i\le1$.
- **Individual-copy configuration relaxation** ([source](#eq:individual-configuration-lp)): Writing $\mathcal H$ for all such subsets, define $$\label{eq:individual-configuration-lp} \operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP,ind}}= \min\left\{\sum_{H\in\mathcal H}y_H: y_H\ge0,\quad \sum_{H\ni i}y_H\ge1\quad(i\in\mathcal I)\right\}.$$
- **Vertex cover** ([source](#pm-h-6)): A vertex cover of a graph is a set of vertices containing at least one endpoint of every edge.
- **Tree depth** ([source](#sec:competing-trees)): In a rooted tree $T$, the depth of a vertex is its distance from the root; write $V_\ell(T)$ for the set of vertices at depth $\ell$.
- **Marking and path hit** ([source](#sec:competing-trees)): A *marking* is a set of vertices at positive depths. It *hits* a path if the path contains a marked vertex.
- **Incidence position** ([source](#pm-h-16)): A *position* is a pair $r=(e,j)$ with $1\le j\le R$; positions are ordered lexicographically.
- **Half-open test intervals** ([source](#pm-h-16)): The *test intervals* are the leaf cells of both trees and all the job cells, each taken left-closed and right-open.
- **Physical tuple and table tuple** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A *tuple* consists of five distinct physical item copies. A *table tuple* has one of the displayed subclass patterns.
- **Good table tuple** ([source](#pm-h-17)): A table tuple is *good* when these three labels are equal.
- **Completion coordinate** ([source](#stmt-lem-good-feasibility)): *For kind $\mathrm M$, with row baseline $b$, deadline $z$, and associated global and local items $U,D$, define $$\label{eq:completion-coordinate} h=b-\Delta\mathbf{1}\{\text{row is short}\}-\operatorname{len}(U)-\operatorname{len}(D).$$
- **Global funded-side counts** ([source](#sec:soundness)): Write $x_v^+$ and $x_v^-$ for the numbers of these tuples using, respectively, a length-one $\mathrm{U}_{+}$ or $\mathrm{U}_{-}$ item.
- **Excluded job position** ([source](#pm-h-35)): For each label $v$, exclude a position $r\in\mathcal R_v$ if its closed job cell $[b_r-\Delta,b_r]$ meets a coverage interval from a good $\mathrm M$ tuple of that label with global length zero and positive local length.
- **Exact-position long-match count** ([source](#pm-h-35)): For $r\in\mathcal R_v$, let $L_{v,r}$ be the number of good $\mathrm G$ tuples whose key is at $r$ and whose row is a long row of that same position.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-primary-score-ceiling | P_0 | = | $100^{11}$ | A fixed score ceiling used throughout the construction. |
| q-additive-pattern-loss-allowance | K | = | $25(c+1)(1+5P_0)$ | Depends on the fixed additive allowance and the primary-score ceiling. |
| q-required-tree-hit-scale | d | ≥ | $\max\{72K,120K/\rho\}$ | Chosen once for the fixed reduction parameters; the tolerance is not a runtime input. |
| q-incidence-repetition-count | R | = | $100(P+nK+1)$ | Depends on vertex count and fixed tree and loss parameters. |
| q-target-bin-and-role-count | B | = | $\sum_{v=1}^n(t_v+2J_v)$ | The target equals the inventory of each role. |
| q-item-size-lower-bound | a_i | > | $1/6$ | Every constructed physical item copy. |
| q-primary-loss-strict-bound | 25c(1+5P_0) | < | $K$ | The fixed additive allowance and prescribed loss constant. |
| q-local-anchor-defect-budget | Anchors outside good tuples at a vertex label | ≤ | $3K$ | A bound per vertex label, independent of its number of positions. |
| q-test-coverage-lower-bound | I_v(a) | ≥ | $d-6K$ | Every test point of every vertex label. |
| q-nonexcluded-long-match-demand | L_{v,r} | ≥ | $d-x_v^+-9K$ | Every nonexcluded incidence position in the fixed packing. |
| q-extracted-cover-size | \|C\| | ≤ | $k+\rho n$ | The prescribed tree scale and fixed extraction tolerance. |
| q-clique-cover-contradiction | k+\rho n | = | $\frac52$ | The cover target and tolerance chosen in the unconditional-gap proof. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-build-competing-trees** [claim](#stmt-lem-competing-trees) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-competing-trees): Build colored spines and surplus continuation chains; use disjoint-branch counting to find an unhit path, prune by color counts, and project a hypothetical combined marking back to the auxiliary tree. -> Separate multiple-hit markings and a shared-quota obstruction.
- **step-prove-geometry-and-order** [claim](#stmt-lem-geometry) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-geometry): Compare nested widths and sibling gaps, then calculate successive position differences and edge offsets independently. -> Separated cells, disjoint tests, and independent coordinate-order bounds.
- **step-encode-scalar-patterns** [claim](#stmt-lem-encoding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-encoding): Decode bounded signed radix totals, classify the resulting role equations, and compare the primary, secondary, and coordinate perturbation scales. -> A cardinality bound and exact score-based feasibility rules.
- **step-bound-good-anchor-defects** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-anchors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-anchors): Use unused slots and primary-score conservation to bound non-table items; use equal label inventories and prefix conservation to bound unequal-label imports. -> A global table-pattern loss bound and a joint anchor-loss bound per label.
- **step-characterise-good-tuple-feasibility** [claim](#stmt-lem-good-feasibility) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-good-feasibility): Sum the coordinate terms of good tuples and apply each independent order inequality to exclude later positions and later-edge resources. -> Deadline feasibility, half-open prefix decomposition, and key-resource restrictions.
- **step-pack-from-vertex-cover** [claim](#stmt-prop-completeness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-completeness): Fund the cover-selected side, allocate separate tree markings on the other side, sort completions against deadlines, and assign remaining exact-edge resources according to endpoint states. -> A partition of all physical items into the target number of feasible bins.
- **step-build-exact-fractional-mixture** [claim](#stmt-prop-fractional) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-fractional): Average both local states, distribute each template weight over physical global and flag copies, project to numerical types, and certify optimality by the cardinality dual. -> Equal target values for both configuration relaxations.
- **step-force-test-coverage** [claim](#stmt-lem-coverage) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coverage): Lower-bound completions using shifted deadlines, upper-bound available baselines using earlier-key consumption, and subtract the bounds through the half-open prefix identity. -> A lower coverage bound at every test point.
- **step-force-funded-side** [claim](#stmt-lem-side-choice) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-side-choice): Convert short local coverage intervals into depth-quota markings; if both global allocations were insufficient, coverage would hit both trees contrary to their shared-quota obstruction. -> A sufficiently large funded allocation on at least one side of every vertex.
- **step-count-displaced-and-long-rows** [claim](#stmt-lem-job-matches) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-job-matches): At a nonexcluded job, subtract displaced current-key rows from the earlier baseline prefix, bound coverage by funded plus tuples and current short rows, and count the remaining exact-position long matches. -> A lower bound on exact-position long key matches.
- **step-certify-threshold-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-edge-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-edge-cover): Conserve earlier-edge resources globally and compare permit capacity with the repeated long-match demand that would arise at an edge with both endpoints below threshold. -> The funded-plus threshold set covers every graph edge.
- **step-bound-threshold-cover-size** [claim](#stmt-lem-cover-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-size): Bound large plus allocations by their inventory, charge one forced side at every vertex, bound the uncharged plus mass, and apply the prescribed tree-scale inequalities. -> A cover within the target plus the prescribed tolerance.
- **step-establish-packing-soundness** [claim](#stmt-prop-soundness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-size-2): Combine threshold edge coverage with its cardinality bound for the stipulated arbitrary packing. -> The soundness implication for every packing within the allowance.
- **step-establish-polynomial-reduction** [claim](#stmt-thm-reduction) / [proof](#pm-h-24): Keep tree parameters fixed, enumerate all incidence positions and physical copies, bound common-denominator bit lengths, and combine completeness with soundness. -> The deterministic polynomial-time graph-to-packing interface.
- **step-deduce-unbounded-gap** [claim](#stmt-thm-unbounded-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-size-3): Apply the reduction to the clique whose minimum cover exceeds the soundness allowance, and independently apply the fractional mixture to certify both relaxation values. -> An unconditional configuration gap for every prescribed additive allowance.
- **step-deduce-fixed-additive-hardness** [claim](#stmt-thm-additive-hardness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-size-4): Transfer a perfect-completeness satisfiability gap to the literal-slot clause graph, identify its cover gap, and choose the fixed extraction tolerance smaller than that gap. -> The hard distinction between target-bin packing and packing beyond the additive allowance.
- **step-deduce-complexity-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-cor-complexity-equiv) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cover-size-5): Use a hypothetical constant-additive algorithm to distinguish the hard promises; conversely use exact capacity verification, optimum decision search, and successive feasible item assignments under the complexity-class equality. -> The equivalence between deterministic constant-additive approximation and the stated complexity-class equality.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-completion-prefix**: For row–auxiliary assignments with completion no later than baseline, the completion prefix equals the baseline prefix plus coverage by the half-open intervals, counting multiplicities.
- **inv-good-tuple-feasibility**: Equal-label table tuples have vanishing primary and secondary scores; their feasibility reduces to the coordinate inequality.
- **inv-full-packing-extraction**: The reduction's cover-extraction guarantee concerns arbitrary packings within the prescribed additive allowance, including packings with defective tuples.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-closed-right-endpoint**: Count coverage using intervals closed at both endpoints. -> An interval contributes coverage at its baseline even though that baseline is already counted in the baseline prefix.
- **anti-reversed-completion**: Apply the prefix decomposition to an assignment whose completion exceeds its baseline. -> A baseline can enter the prefix before its completion, invalidating the partition of completion events.
- **anti-assume-every-bin-good**: Treat every feasible bin in an arbitrary packing as an equal-label table tuple. -> The completion interpretation can be applied to bins that lack the required pattern or label equality.
- **anti-prefix-test-as-gap-proof**: Use successful finite prefix-count tests as certification of the unbounded configuration gap or additive hardness. -> The tests do not construct the competing trees, encoded inventories, fractional columns, or extracted covers.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. This executable component implements the finite rational prefix-count identity underlying the completion argument, rather than the full graph-to-packing construction. The source states: The last term counts coverage by the half-open intervals $[h,b)$.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with a rational point a and a list of rational pairs [h, b]; name is optional metadata.
# PRECONDITIONS: Each rational is an integer or numerator/denominator string; each h <= b.
# OUTPUTS: JSON integer counts baselines, completions, and coverage, preserving multiplicities.
# TIME: Linear in the number of pairs, with exact rational comparison costs.
# SPACE: Linear in the input length, including stored exact rationals.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic with flint.fmpq; no floating-point conversion.
# FAILURE: Malformed rational data or reversed endpoints raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.
import json
import sys
from flint import fmpq

def rational(text):
    if not isinstance(text, str):
        raise ValueError('rational strings required')
    parts = text.split('/')
    if len(parts) == 1:
        return fmpq(int(parts[0]))
    if len(parts) == 2:
        return fmpq(int(parts[0]), int(parts[1]))
    raise ValueError('invalid rational')

def solve(data):
    a = rational(data['a'])
    pairs = []
    for pair in data['pairs']:
        if not isinstance(pair, list) or len(pair) != 2:
            raise ValueError('endpoint pair required')
        h, b = map(rational, pair)
        if h > b:
            raise ValueError('completion exceeds baseline')
        pairs.append((h, b))
    baselines = sum(1 for h, b in pairs if b <= a)
    completions = sum(1 for h, b in pairs if h <= a)
    coverage = sum(1 for h, b in pairs if h <= a < b)
    return {'baselines': baselines, 'completions': completions, 'coverage': coverage}

print(json.dumps(solve(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))
```

    "oracle_code": "import bisect\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\ndef main():\n    with open(sys.argv[1], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        data = json.load(stream)\n    with open(sys.argv[2], encoding='utf-8') as stream:\n        observed = json.load(stream)\n    expected = json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    a = Fraction(data['a'])\n    starts = []\n    ends = []\n    for pair in data['pairs']:\n        if not isinstance(pair, list) or len(pair) != 2:\n            return 1\n        h, b = (Fraction(value) for value in pair)\n        if h > b:\n            return 1\n        starts.append(h)\n        ends.append(b)\n    starts.sort()\n    ends.sort()\n    entered = bisect.bisect_right(starts, a)\n    departed = bisect.bisect_right(ends, a)\n    truth = {'baselines': departed, 'completions': entered, 'coverage': entered - departed}\n    if not isinstance(observed, dict) or set(observed) != set(truth):\n        return 1\n    if any(type(value) is not int for value in observed.values()):\n        return 1\n    if expected != truth or observed != truth:\n        return 1\n    if observed['completions'] != observed['baselines'] + observed['coverage']:\n        return 1\n    return 0\n\ntry:\n    sys.exit(main())\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(1)\n",
    "output_contract": "A JSON object contains exactly baselines, completions, and coverage. They count b <= a, h <= a, and h <= a < b respectively, with multiplicity. The oracle checks each count independently through sorted endpoint ranks and checks completions = baselines + coverage.",
    "reason": "This executable component implements the finite rational prefix-count identity underlying the completion argument, rather than the full graph-to-packing construction. The source states: The last term counts coverage by the half-open intervals $[h,b)$.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with a rational point a and a list of rational pairs [h, b]; name is optional metadata.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Each rational is an integer or numerator/denominator string; each h <= b.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON integer counts baselines, completions, and coverage, preserving multiplicities.\n# TIME: Linear in the number of pairs, with exact rational comparison costs.\n# SPACE: Linear in the input length, including stored exact rationals.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic with flint.fmpq; no floating-point conversion.\n# FAILURE: Malformed rational data or reversed endpoints raises an exception and exits unsuccessfully.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndef rational(text):\n    if not isinstance(text, str):\n        raise ValueError('rational strings required')\n    parts = text.split('/')\n    if len(parts) == 1:\n        return fmpq(int(parts[0]))\n    if len(parts) == 2:\n        return fmpq(int(parts[0]), int(parts[1]))\n    raise ValueError('invalid rational')\n\ndef solve(data):\n    a = rational(data['a'])\n    pairs = []\n    for pair in data['pairs']:\n        if not isinstance(pair, list) or len(pair) != 2:\n            raise ValueError('endpoint pair required')\n        h, b = map(rational, pair)\n        if h > b:\n            raise ValueError('completion exceeds baseline')\n        pairs.append((h, b))\n    baselines = sum(1 for h, b in pairs if b <= a)\n    completions = sum(1 for h, b in pairs if h <= a)\n    coverage = sum(1 for h, b in pairs if h <= a < b)\n    return {'baselines': baselines, 'completions': completions, 'coverage': coverage}\n\nprint(json.dumps(solve(json.load(sys.stdin)), sort_keys=True))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The explicit graph-to-packing construction can generate adversarial inputs for packing software. Its completeness and soundness implications provide mathematical expectations for these inputs, although the fixed construction parameters can make practical enumeration prohibitively large. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The interval cells encode packing constraints and coverage demands; the paper supplies no game mechanics, simulation evolution, or numerical integration contract. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The certificate description gives a concrete packing-validation contract: a proposed assignment is checked against all exact rational capacity constraints. This validates a supplied packing without establishing its optimality. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | stated | The paper gives a deterministic reduction from explicit simple graphs to rational packing instances and a constructive packing procedure from a vertex cover. It also establishes a hardness obstruction to deterministic constant-additive approximation. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The rooted trees and indexed copies are combinatorial gadgets used in the reduction. The paper gives no storage representation, update interface, or data-structure performance contract. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The construction is analyzed through binary encoding length and exact arithmetic. It provides no accelerator architecture, parallel execution scheme, or hardware resource bound. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The promised packing distinction is an ordinary worst-case hardness result. No key generation, cryptographic primitive, adversarial security experiment, or average-case assumption is constructed. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The fractional witness averages feasible local configurations to meet item inventories. This is a covering-LP construction, with no learning objective, training procedure, or prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The paper constructs a primal fractional packing and a matching cardinality-based dual certificate for both configuration relaxations. Together with cover extraction, these give finite witnesses of additive integrality gaps and constrain constant-additive packing guarantees. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The inventory and prefix conservation arguments concern a single mathematical packing of distinct item copies. They specify no communication protocol, distributed consistency mechanism, or failure-recovery contract. |
| other (Combinatorial path-hitting constructions) | True | strong | implementable | stated | The competing-tree lemma supplies an explicit finite obstruction for shared depth quotas. Each tree has its own multiple-hit marking, while the combined quotas prevent simultaneous path hitting. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-explicit-rational-input-model** (formal_scope): The algorithmic guarantees concern explicitly listed rational items and running time measured against their binary encoding length. Blocked use: ['Transfer the polynomial-time guarantees directly to a compressed multiplicity representation or an input containing arbitrary real sizes.']
- **limit-fixed-reduction-parameters** (hypothesis): Polynomial construction is established separately for fixed additive allowance and positive extraction tolerance. The finite tree construction is part of those fixed parameters. Blocked use: ['Claim a uniform polynomial construction when the allowance or extraction tolerance varies as part of the runtime input.', 'Treat the extraction tolerance as an arbitrary real-valued input.']
- **limit-simple-nonempty-graph-interface** (hypothesis): The reduction interface requires an explicit simple graph with a nonempty edge set and a cover target within the vertex-count range. Isolated vertices are accommodated by the completeness construction. Blocked use: ['Apply the stated reduction interface directly to a graph with no edges, a multigraph, or an out-of-range cover target.', 'Reject an otherwise admissible graph merely because it contains isolated vertices.']
- **limit-tree-depth-and-marking-preservation** (hypothesis): The competing-tree obstruction uses preserved original depths and arbitrary positive-depth markings. Finite constructibility alone supplies no efficient bound when the marking demand varies. Blocked use: ['Contract edges during pruning while retaining the original quota interpretation.', 'Restrict the shared-quota obstruction to colored vertices.', 'Infer a polynomial bound in the marking demand from finite deterministic constructibility.']
- **limit-half-open-tests-and-closed-exclusion-cells** (edge_case): Test coverage uses left-closed, right-open intervals, while geometric cells used for containment and exclusion remain closed. These conventions have different roles at tied endpoints. Blocked use: ['Count coverage at a baseline endpoint.', 'Replace the closed-cell exclusion test with a half-open-cell test without re-establishing its conservative intersection property.']
- **limit-scalar-encoding-does-not-recover-metadata** (formal_scope): The analysis retains item roles, labels, and physical indices as construction metadata. Numerical sizes need not distinguish declared species or permit recovery of that metadata. Blocked use: ['Decode a unique role or vertex label from an item size.', 'Merge distinct physical copies merely because their numerical sizes coincide.', 'Require different declared species to have different numerical sizes.']
- **limit-good-tuple-coordinate-interpretation** (hypothesis): The completion and key interpretations require good table tuples. A feasible arbitrary bin need not have the intended pattern or equal labels; departures must be accounted for separately. Blocked use: ['Apply the coordinate-only feasibility rule to every feasible bin.', 'Infer label equality from zero primary score alone.', 'Assume that every packing consists exclusively of intended tuples.']
- **limit-additive-packing-loss-regime** (hypothesis): The defect bounds and cover extraction apply to the prescribed constructed inventory and packings within the stipulated additive allowance. The anchor bound is joint across deadlines and keys at a label. Blocked use: ['Reuse the defect budget for a packing beyond the prescribed allowance.', 'Transfer the defect bounds to an arbitrary rational packing instance.', 'Charge the joint anchor loss independently at every job position.']
- **limit-completeness-needs-supplied-cover** (hypothesis): The integral completeness procedure constructs a packing from an available vertex cover satisfying the cover target. It does not provide an unconditional method for finding that cover. Blocked use: ['Use the completeness procedure as a polynomial-time solver for minimum vertex cover.', 'Assume that arbitrary local selected and unselected states exhaust the edge inventories feasibly.']
- **limit-fractional-clique-and-physical-copy-witness** (hypothesis): The exact fractional witness is established for the prescribed four-vertex clique construction. Its columns require distinct physical copies and nonempty requested pools, and their projection preserves numerical-type demand. Blocked use: ['Assert the same exact fractional objective for every input graph.', 'Generate a column using the same physical copy in different roles.', 'Allocate uniform fractional weights over an empty requested pool.']
- **limit-fractional-states-not-integrally-compatible** (approximation): Fractional resource averaging does not establish that the local states can be chosen simultaneously as an integral packing. The integral lower bound is proved independently through soundness. Blocked use: ['Round the fractional local-state mixture by choosing states independently and claim the same bin count.', 'Treat matching fractional inventory demands as an integral feasibility certificate.']
- **limit-nonexcluded-long-match-bound** (hypothesis): The long-match lower bound concerns nonexcluded positions and exact-position long rows. Displaced key matches are permitted and must be included in the prefix accounting. Blocked use: ['Apply the nonexcluded-position lower bound at an excluded job.', 'Assume every good key is matched to a row at its own position.', 'Count displaced or short rows as exact-position long matches.']
- **limit-threshold-cover-resource-accounting** (hypothesis): The threshold-cover proof uses current-edge permits together with residual earlier-edge resources. Its size bound charges a single forced side at each vertex, even when both sides are large. Blocked use: ['Require every exact-position long match to use a permit of its own edge.', 'Ignore earlier-edge resources when bounding edge capacity.', 'Charge both funded sides at a vertex in the cover-cardinality argument.']
- **limit-gap-growth-not-logarithmic-size-lower-bound** (unknown): The paper establishes arbitrarily large additive gaps but does not establish a matching logarithmic lower bound as a function of instance size. Blocked use: ['Report the construction as a matching size-dependent logarithmic integrality-gap lower bound.', 'Infer practical benchmark sizes from the existence of a prescribed additive gap.']
- **limit-bin-cardinality-not-fixed-size-types** (formal_scope): Bounded bin cardinality does not bound the number of distinct numerical sizes. The hardness result therefore does not resolve the fixed-size-type parameter regime. Blocked use: ['Transfer the hardness result to instances with a fixed number of numerical size types.', 'Claim a contradiction with exact algorithms in the fixed-type regime.']
- **limit-hardness-premise-and-deterministic-equivalence** (formal_scope): The structural gap is unconditional. Algorithmic hardness additionally uses a satisfiability promise with perfect completeness, and the approximation equivalence is stated for deterministic polynomial-time algorithms. Blocked use: ['Make the structural gap conditional on a separation of complexity classes.', 'Replace the satisfiability promise with a premise lacking perfect completeness without repairing the reduction.', 'Extend the deterministic approximation equivalence to randomized algorithms without a separate argument.']
- **limit-conditional-optimal-packing-search** (nonconstructive): The optimal-packing search in the reverse complexity implication relies on the assumed polynomial solvability of decision problems, including problems with partial assignments already fixed. Blocked use: ['Publish the reverse implication as an unconditional polynomial-time optimal packing algorithm.', 'Replace the partial-assignment feasibility test with ordinary feasibility while claiming that successive assignments preserve a feasible completion.']
- **limit-prefix-component-certification-scope** (formal_scope): Exact finite rational prefix-count checks address the completion identity and its endpoint conventions. They do not establish the full tree construction, scalar encoding, fractional optimum, cover extraction, or complexity hardness. Blocked use: ['Treat successful finite prefix examples as a certificate of the universal main theorems.', 'Transfer executable coverage of the prefix identity to the untested good-tuple and full-packing extraction invariants.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 118 (`lean/docs/118.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Additive hardness and unbounded configuration gaps in bin packing](../../preprints/Additive-hardness-and-unbounded-configuration-gaps-in-bin-packing-September-24-2026/Additive-hardness-and-unbounded-configuration-gaps-in-bin-packing-September-24-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized results rule out a universal additive bound for the configuration linear program in bin packing. For every integer $c\ge0$, there are an integer $B$ and a rational instance with $5B$ items whose individual-copy and size-type configuration-LP values both equal $B$, but whose integral optimum is greater than $B+c$. Distinguishing a packing in $B$ bins from the absence of one in $B+c$ bins is NP-hard for each fixed $c$. A deterministic polynomial-time algorithm with a fixed absolute additive allowance exists exactly when $P=NP$; under that equality, an optimal algorithm is constructed.

import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

namespace BinPackingGames.Foundations.Complexity

def encodeWord (n : Nat) : List Bool := List.replicate n true ++ [false]

end BinPackingGames.Foundations.Complexity

namespace BinPackingGames.Foundations.Complexity.MachineFiniteAlphabet

open Turing

def FiniteAlphabet (M : FinTM2) : Prop := ∀ k, Finite (M.Γ k)

end BinPackingGames.Foundations.Complexity.MachineFiniteAlphabet

namespace BinPackingGames.Foundations.Complexity.CookLevin

def pairBits (input : List Bool × List Bool) : List Bool :=
  encodeWord input.1.length ++ input.1 ++ input.2

structure NPVerifier where
  witnessBound : Polynomial Nat
  verify : (List Bool × List Bool) → Bool
  computation : Turing.TM2ComputableInPolyTime pairBits (fun bit => [bit]) verify
  finiteAlphabet : (k : computation.tm.K) → Fintype (computation.tm.Γ k)

def NPVerifier.Accepts (verifier : NPVerifier) (input : List Bool) : Prop :=
  ∃ witness : List Bool,
    witness.length ≤ verifier.witnessBound.eval input.length ∧
    verifier.verify (input, witness) = true

def InNP (language : List Bool → Prop) : Prop :=
  ∃ verifier : NPVerifier, ∀ input, language input ↔ verifier.Accepts input

end BinPackingGames.Foundations.Complexity.CookLevin

namespace BinPackingCompleteness.BinaryEncoding

def bitsValue : List Bool → Nat
  | [] => 0
  | b :: bits => Nat.bit b (bitsValue bits)

def frame : List Bool → List Bool
  | [] => [false]
  | b :: bits => true :: b :: frame bits

def parseFrame : List Bool → Option (List Bool × List Bool)
  | false :: rest => some ([], rest)
  | true :: b :: rest => do
      let (bits, trailing) ← parseFrame rest
      return (b :: bits, trailing)
  | _ => none

def nameBits (name : Nat) : List Bool := frame name.bits

def parseName (input : List Bool) : Option (Nat × List Bool) := do
  let (digits, rest) ← parseFrame input
  let name := bitsValue digits
  if digits = name.bits then some (name, rest) else none

end BinPackingCompleteness.BinaryEncoding

namespace BinPackingGap

open scoped BigOperators

structure Instance where
  n : ℕ
  size : Fin n → ℚ
  size_pos : ∀ i, 0 < size i
  size_le_one : ∀ i, size i ≤ 1

namespace Instance

abbrev Item (I : Instance) := Fin I.n

end Instance

structure Packing (I : Instance) (b : ℕ) where
  assignment : I.Item → Fin b
  capacity : ∀ j, (∑ i, if assignment i = j then I.size i else 0) ≤ 1

def HasPacking (I : Instance) (b : ℕ) : Prop := Nonempty (Packing I b)

def singletonPacking (I : Instance) : Packing I I.n where
  assignment i := i
  capacity j := by
    have hsum : (∑ i, if i = j then I.size i else 0) = I.size j := by
      calc
        (∑ i, if i = j then I.size i else 0) =
            (if j = j then I.size j else 0) :=
          Finset.sum_eq_single j (fun i _ hij => ite_eq_right hij)
            (fun h => False.elim (h (Finset.mem_univ _)))
        _ = I.size j := ite_eq_left rfl
    rw [hsum]
    exact I.size_le_one j

theorem exists_packing (I : Instance) : ∃ b, HasPacking I b :=
  ⟨I.n, ⟨singletonPacking I⟩⟩

noncomputable def opt (I : Instance) : ℕ := by
  classical
  exact Nat.find (exists_packing I)

def IndividualConfiguration (I : Instance) :=
  {H : Finset I.Item // ∑ i ∈ H, I.size i ≤ 1}

noncomputable instance (I : Instance) : Fintype (IndividualConfiguration I) :=
  inferInstanceAs (Fintype {H : Finset I.Item // ∑ i ∈ H, I.size i ≤ 1})

def NumericalSize (I : Instance) := ↥(Finset.univ.image I.size)

instance (I : Instance) : Fintype (NumericalSize I) :=
  inferInstanceAs (Fintype ↥(Finset.univ.image I.size))

namespace NumericalSize

variable {I : Instance}

theorem positive (s : NumericalSize I) : 0 < s.val := by
  obtain ⟨i, _, hi⟩ := Finset.mem_image.mp s.property
  rw [← hi]
  exact I.size_pos i

def multiplicity (s : NumericalSize I) : ℕ :=
  (Finset.univ.filter fun i : I.Item => I.size i = s.val).card

def capacityBound (s : NumericalSize I) : ℕ :=
  Classical.choose (exists_nat_gt (1 / s.val))

theorem capacityBound_spec (s : NumericalSize I) : 1 / s.val < (s.capacityBound : ℚ) :=
  Classical.choose_spec (exists_nat_gt (1 / s.val))

end NumericalSize

def TypeConfiguration (I : Instance) :=
  {c : NumericalSize I → ℕ // ∑ s, s.val * (c s : ℚ) ≤ 1}

namespace TypeConfiguration

variable {I : Instance}

theorem count_lt_bound (c : TypeConfiguration I) (s : NumericalSize I) :
    c.val s < s.capacityBound := by
  have hterm : s.val * (c.val s : ℚ) ≤ ∑ t, t.val * (c.val t : ℚ) := by
    exact Finset.single_le_sum (fun t _ => mul_nonneg (le_of_lt t.positive) (Nat.cast_nonneg _))
      (Finset.mem_univ s)
  have hquot : (c.val s : ℚ) ≤ 1 / s.val := by
    apply (le_div_iff₀ s.positive).mpr
    simpa [mul_comm] using hterm.trans c.property
  have hlt := hquot.trans_lt s.capacityBound_spec
  exact_mod_cast hlt

def boundedCounts (c : TypeConfiguration I) : ∀ s : NumericalSize I, Fin s.capacityBound :=
  fun s => ⟨c.val s, c.count_lt_bound s⟩

theorem boundedCounts_injective :
    Function.Injective
      (boundedCounts : TypeConfiguration I → ∀ s : NumericalSize I, Fin s.capacityBound) := by
  intro c d h
  apply Subtype.ext
  funext s
  exact congrArg Fin.val (congrFun h s)

instance : Finite (TypeConfiguration I) := by
  classical
  exact Finite.of_injective boundedCounts boundedCounts_injective

instance : Fintype (TypeConfiguration I) := Fintype.ofFinite _

end TypeConfiguration

namespace FractionalCover

open Set
variable {I C : Type*} [Fintype I] [Fintype C]

def Feasible (a : C → I → ℝ) (d : I → ℝ) (w : C → ℝ) : Prop :=
  (∀ c, 0 ≤ w c) ∧ ∀ i, d i ≤ ∑ c, a c i * w c

def objective (w : C → ℝ) : ℝ := ∑ c, w c

def objectiveSet (a : C → I → ℝ) (d : I → ℝ) : Set ℝ :=
  {v | ∃ w, Feasible a d w ∧ objective w = v}

def value (a : C → I → ℝ) (d : I → ℝ) : ℝ :=
  sInf (objectiveSet a d)

end FractionalCover

def individualMatrix (I : Instance) (H : IndividualConfiguration I) (i : I.Item) : ℝ :=
  if i ∈ H.val then 1 else 0

def individualLP (I : Instance) : ℝ :=
  FractionalCover.value (individualMatrix I) (fun _ => 1)

def typeMatrix (I : Instance) (c : TypeConfiguration I) (s : NumericalSize I) : ℝ :=
  c.val s

def typeLP (I : Instance) : ℝ :=
  FractionalCover.value (typeMatrix I) (fun s => (s.multiplicity : ℝ))

abbrev RawInstance := List (ℕ × ℕ)

abbrev RawReductionOutput := ℕ × RawInstance

namespace RawInstance

def Valid (r : RawInstance) : Prop :=
  ∀ q ∈ r, 0 < q.1 ∧ q.1 ≤ q.2

def ratValue (q : ℕ × ℕ) : ℚ := (q.1 : ℚ) / (q.2 : ℚ)

theorem den_pos {r : RawInstance} (hr : r.Valid) {q : ℕ × ℕ} (hq : q ∈ r) :
    0 < q.2 := lt_of_lt_of_le (hr q hq).1 (hr q hq).2

def toInstance (r : RawInstance) (hr : r.Valid) : Instance where
  n := r.length
  size i := ratValue (r.get i)
  size_pos i := div_pos (Nat.cast_pos.mpr (hr _ (List.get_mem r i)).1)
    (Nat.cast_pos.mpr (den_pos hr (List.get_mem r i)))
  size_le_one i := (div_le_one₀ (Nat.cast_pos.mpr (den_pos hr (List.get_mem r i)))).mpr
    (Nat.cast_le.mpr (hr _ (List.get_mem r i)).2)

end RawInstance

structure RawPacking where
  bins : ℕ
  assignments : List ℕ

namespace RawPacking

def load (r : RawInstance) (a : List ℕ) (j : ℕ) : ℚ :=
  ((r.zip a).map fun qa =>
    if qa.2 = j then RawInstance.ratValue qa.1 else 0).sum

def Feasible (r : RawInstance) (p : RawPacking) : Prop :=
  p.assignments.length = r.length ∧
  (∀ j ∈ p.assignments, j < p.bins) ∧
  ∀ j < p.bins, load r p.assignments j ≤ 1

end RawPacking

namespace BinaryEncoding

abbrev natBits := BinPackingCompleteness.BinaryEncoding.nameBits

abbrev parseNat := BinPackingCompleteness.BinaryEncoding.parseName

def listBits {α : Type} (encode : α → List Bool) : List α → List Bool
  | [] => [false]
  | a :: rest => true :: (encode a ++ listBits encode rest)

def pairBits {α β : Type} (ea : α → List Bool) (eb : β → List Bool)
    (p : α × β) : List Bool := ea p.1 ++ eb p.2

def optionBits {α : Type} (encode : α → List Bool) : Option α → List Bool
  | none => [false]
  | some a => true :: encode a

abbrev Parser (α : Type) := List Bool → Option (α × List Bool)

def parsePair {α β : Type} (pa : Parser α) (pb : Parser β) : Parser (α × β) :=
  fun input => do
    let (a, rest) ← pa input
    let (b, suffix) ← pb rest
    return ((a, b), suffix)

def parseListAux {α : Type} (parse : Parser α) : Nat → Parser (List α)
  | 0, _ => none
  | _ + 1, [] => none
  | _ + 1, false :: rest => some ([], rest)
  | fuel + 1, true :: rest => do
    let (a, rest) ← parse rest
    let (as, suffix) ← parseListAux parse fuel rest
    return (a :: as, suffix)

def parseList {α : Type} (parse : Parser α) : Parser (List α) :=
  fun input => parseListAux parse input.length input

def decode {α : Type} (parse : Parser α) (input : List Bool) : Option α := do
  let (a, rest) ← parse input
  if rest.isEmpty then some a else none

def rawInstanceBits (r : RawInstance) : List Bool :=
  listBits (pairBits natBits natBits) r

def assignmentBits (a : List Nat) : List Bool := listBits natBits a

def rawPackingBits (p : RawPacking) : List Bool :=
  natBits p.bins ++ assignmentBits p.assignments

def packingResultBits (p : Option RawPacking) : List Bool := optionBits rawPackingBits p

def rawReductionOutputBits (out : RawReductionOutput) : List Bool :=
  natBits out.1 ++ rawInstanceBits out.2

def parseRawInstance : Parser RawInstance := parseList (parsePair parseNat parseNat)

def decodeRawInstance := decode parseRawInstance

end BinaryEncoding

section

open Turing BinPackingGames.Foundations.Complexity

abbrev BitLanguage := List Bool → Prop

def InP (L : BitLanguage) : Prop :=
  ∃ decider : List Bool → Bool,
  ∃ computation : TM2ComputableInPolyTime
      (id : List Bool → List Bool) (fun b => [b]) decider,
    MachineFiniteAlphabet.FiniteAlphabet computation.tm ∧
    ∀ x, L x ↔ decider x = true

def ClassP : Set BitLanguage := {L | InP L}

def ClassNP : Set BitLanguage := {L | CookLevin.InNP L}

def PEqualsNP : Prop := ClassP = ClassNP

structure AbsoluteAdditiveAlgorithm (c : Nat) where
  run : List Bool → Option RawPacking
  computation : TM2ComputableInPolyTime (id : List Bool → List Bool)
    BinaryEncoding.packingResultBits run
  finiteAlphabet : MachineFiniteAlphabet.FiniteAlphabet computation.tm
  guarantee : ∀ (r : RawInstance) (hr : r.Valid), ∃ p : RawPacking,
    run (BinaryEncoding.rawInstanceBits r) = some p ∧
    RawPacking.Feasible r p ∧ p.bins ≤ opt (r.toInstance hr) + c

structure PackingGapReduction (c : Nat) (L : BitLanguage) where
  reduce : List Bool → RawReductionOutput
  computation : TM2ComputableInPolyTime (id : List Bool → List Bool)
    BinaryEncoding.rawReductionOutputBits reduce
  finiteAlphabet : MachineFiniteAlphabet.FiniteAlphabet computation.tm
  valid : ∀ x, (reduce x).2.Valid
  itemCount : ∀ x, (reduce x).2.length = 5 * (reduce x).1
  itemLower : ∀ x i,
    (1 / 6 : ℚ) < ((reduce x).2.toInstance (valid x)).size i
  completeness : ∀ x, L x →
    HasPacking ((reduce x).2.toInstance (valid x)) (reduce x).1
  soundness : ∀ x, ¬L x →
    ¬HasPacking ((reduce x).2.toInstance (valid x)) ((reduce x).1 + c)

def PackingGapNPHard (c : Nat) : Prop :=
  ∀ L : BitLanguage, CookLevin.InNP L → Nonempty (PackingGapReduction c L)

end

theorem main_results :
    (∀ c : ℕ, ∃ I : Instance, ∃ B : ℕ,
      I.n = 5 * B ∧
      (∀ i, (1 / 6 : ℚ) < I.size i ∧ I.size i < 1) ∧
      B + c < opt I ∧ individualLP I = (B : ℝ) ∧ typeLP I = (B : ℝ)) ∧
    (∀ c : Nat, PackingGapNPHard c) ∧
    ((∃ c : Nat, Nonempty (AbsoluteAdditiveAlgorithm c)) ↔ PEqualsNP) ∧
    (PEqualsNP → ∃ algorithm : AbsoluteAdditiveAlgorithm 0,
    (∀ (items : RawInstance) (valid : items.Valid), ∃ packing : RawPacking,
      algorithm.run (BinaryEncoding.rawInstanceBits items) = some packing ∧
      packing.Feasible items ∧ packing.bins = opt (items.toInstance valid)) ∧
    algorithm.run (BinaryEncoding.rawInstanceBits []) = some ⟨0, []⟩ ∧
    (∀ input : List Bool, BinaryEncoding.decodeRawInstance input = none →
      algorithm.run input = none) ∧
    (∀ items : RawInstance, ¬ items.Valid →
      algorithm.run (BinaryEncoding.rawInstanceBits items) = none))  := by
  sorry

end BinPackingGap

end

end OAI

    "family": "118",
    "text": "The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):\n\n- [Additive hardness and unbounded configuration gaps in bin packing](../../preprints/Additive-hardness-and-unbounded-configuration-gaps-in-bin-packing-September-24-2026/Additive-hardness-and-unbounded-configuration-gaps-in-bin-packing-September-24-2026.pdf)\n\n## Scope\n\nThe formalized results rule out a universal additive bound for the configuration linear program in bin packing. For every integer $c\\ge0$, there are an integer $B$ and a rational instance with $5B$ items whose individual-copy and size-type configuration-LP values both equal $B$, but whose integral optimum is greater than $B+c$. Distinguishing a packing in $B$ bins from the absence of one in $B+c$ bins is NP-hard for each fixed $c$. A deterministic polynomial-time algorithm with a fixed absolute additive allowance exists exactly when $P=NP$; under that equality, an optimal algorithm is constructed."

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We disprove the Modified Integer Round-Up Conjecture for bin packing by constructing instances with arbitrarily large additive gaps between the configuration-LP value and the integral optimum. We also prove that, for every fixed nonnegative integer <i>c</i>, distinguishing instances that fit in <i>B</i> bins from those requiring more than $`B+c`$ bins is NP-hard. Both results hold with rational item sizes greater than 1/6, so each bin contains at most five items.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

An instance of bin packing is an explicitly listed collection of rational item sizes in $(0,1]$, encoded in binary. A bin may contain any subcollection of total size at most one. Write $\operatorname{OPT}(I)$ for the minimum number of bins needed to pack an instance $I$, and $a_i$ for the size of its $i$th item copy. We consider deterministic algorithms whose running time is polynomial in the length of this explicit encoding. The additive approximation question asks whether one algorithm and one absolute constant $C$ can guarantee at most $\operatorname{OPT}(I)+C$ bins for every instance $I$. Williamson and Shmoys included this constant-additive question among the open problems in their 2011 book on approximation algorithms <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[WilliamsonShmoys2011](#ref-WilliamsonShmoys2011), Chapter 17, Problem 3].

The configuration linear program assigns nonnegative weights to feasible bin patterns and minimizes their total weight subject to covering the item demands. Its cutting-stock antecedents include Eisemann’s formulation and Gilmore and Gomory’s column-generation method <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[GilmoreGomory1961](#ref-GilmoreGomory1961), p. 849]. We use the ordinary formulation in numerical size types. We will also prove the same gap for the stronger formulation whose patterns are subsets of the individual item copies.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Configuration relaxations and integer rounding

<a id="stmt-def-configuration-lp"></a>
<div id="def:configuration-lp" class="definition">

<a id="eq:type-configuration-lp"></a>
**Definition 1** (Configuration relaxations). For the ordinary type formulation, let $\mathcal S$ be the set of distinct numerical sizes and let $n_\sigma$ be the multiplicity of $\sigma\in\mathcal S$. Its configurations are all integer vectors $$\mathcal C=\left\{(c_\sigma)_{\sigma\in\mathcal S}
       \in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}^{\mathcal S}:
       \sum_{\sigma\in\mathcal S}\sigma c_\sigma\le1\right\}.$$ The Gilmore–Gomory relaxation is $$\label{eq:type-configuration-lp}
 \operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP}}=
 \min\left\{\sum_{\mathbf c\in\mathcal C}z_{\mathbf c}:
       z_{\mathbf c}\ge0,\quad
       \sum_{\mathbf c\in\mathcal C}c_\sigma z_{\mathbf c}
          \ge n_\sigma\quad(\sigma\in\mathcal S)\right\}.$$ In particular, type configurations may repeat a size; their multiplicities are limited by capacity, rather than by the input multiplicities.

<a id="eq:individual-configuration-lp"></a>
Give the input items distinct physical indices in a set $\mathcal I$, with size $a_i$ at index $i$, even when some sizes coincide. An individual-copy configuration is a subset $H\subseteq\mathcal I$ with $\sum_{i\in H}a_i\le1$. Writing $\mathcal H$ for all such subsets, define $$\label{eq:individual-configuration-lp}
 \operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP,ind}}=
 \min\left\{\sum_{H\in\mathcal H}y_H:
       y_H\ge0,\quad
       \sum_{H\ni i}y_H\ge1\quad(i\in\mathcal I)\right\}.$$

</div>

Both programs are lower bounds on $\operatorname{OPT}(I)$. The distinction between patterns that may repeat a size beyond its input multiplicity and patterns limited by available copies is relevant to integer-rounding questions; see Kartak, Ripatti, Scheithauer, and Kurz <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KartakRipattiScheithauerKurz2015](#ref-KartakRipattiScheithauerKurz2015)]. Here $\operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP}}(I)$ always denotes the unrestricted numerical-type formulation in Equation <a href="#eq:type-configuration-lp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:type-configuration-lp">[eq:type-configuration-lp]</a>. We prove its value and the individual-copy value separately for our instances.

<a id="eq:mirup"></a>
The integer round-up property asks whether the integral optimum equals the fractional optimum rounded up. Marcotte studied this property for cutting stock and exhibited an instance for which it fails <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Marcotte1985](#ref-Marcotte1985), [Marcotte1986](#ref-Marcotte1986)]. Scheithauer and Terno investigated the modified bound <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[ScheithauerTerno1995](#ref-ScheithauerTerno1995), [ScheithauerTerno1997](#ref-ScheithauerTerno1997)] $$\label{eq:mirup}
  \operatorname{OPT}(I)\le \lceil\operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP}}(I)\rceil+1,$$ now known as the *Modified Integer Round-Up Conjecture*. The algorithmic question and this structural question require separate arguments: Equation <a href="#eq:mirup" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mirup">[eq:mirup]</a> concerns the existence of a packing, whereas an approximation algorithm must construct one efficiently.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Results

Our first result is an unconditional family of gaps for both configuration relaxations.

<a id="stmt-thm-unbounded-gap"></a>
<div id="thm:unbounded-gap" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:main-gap"></a>
**Theorem 2** (Unbounded configuration gap). *For every integer $c\ge0$, there is a finite rational bin-packing instance $I$ and an integer $B$ such that every item size exceeds $1/6$ and $$\label{eq:main-gap}
  \operatorname{OPT}_{\mathrm{LP}}(I)=B,
  \qquad \operatorname{OPT}(I)>B+c.$$ The equality for the fractional optimum also holds for the configuration LP whose patterns are subsets of the individual item copies.*

</div>

Taking $c=1$ contradicts Equation <a href="#eq:mirup" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:mirup">[eq:mirup]</a>, so Theorem <a href="#thm:unbounded-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:unbounded-gap">2</a> disproves the Modified Integer Round-Up Conjecture. The integral LP value in the construction makes this a gap beyond the conjectured rounding allowance, without any ambiguity caused by the ceiling.

The same construction yields hardness for every fixed additive allowance.

<a id="stmt-thm-additive-hardness"></a>
<div id="thm:additive-hardness" class="theorem">

**Theorem 3** (Constant-additive hardness). *For every fixed integer $c\ge0$, it is NP-hard to distinguish pairs $(I,B)$ such that $I$ packs into $B$ bins from pairs such that $I$ cannot be packed into $B+c$ bins. Here $B$ is an integer supplied with the instance, the item sizes are rational, and every item size is strictly greater than $1/6$.*

</div>

<a id="stmt-cor-complexity-equiv"></a>
<div id="cor:complexity-equiv" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4**. *A deterministic polynomial-time algorithm that always uses at most $\operatorname{OPT}(I)+C$ bins for some absolute constant $C$ exists if and only if $\mathsf P=\mathsf{NP}$.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:unbounded-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:unbounded-gap">2</a> assumes no separation of complexity classes. Theorem <a href="#thm:additive-hardness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:additive-hardness">3</a> gives the conditional negative answer to the algorithmic question through Corollary <a href="#cor:complexity-equiv" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:complexity-equiv">4</a>. All constructions use sizes greater than $1/6$, so each feasible bin contains at most five items.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Approximation bounds and related constructions

Fernandez de la Vega and Lueker used grouping of item sizes to obtain an asymptotic approximation scheme <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[FernandezDeLaVegaLueker1981](#ref-FernandezDeLaVegaLueker1981)]: for each fixed positive $\varepsilon$, the multiplicative ratio can be made $1+\varepsilon$ once the optimum is sufficiently large. A fixed multiplicative excess still allows an additive error that grows with the optimum. Karmarkar and Karp combined grouping with approximate solution of the configuration LP and repeated packing of its integral part, obtaining an additive bound of order $\log^2\operatorname{OPT}$ <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KarmarkarKarp1982](#ref-KarmarkarKarp1982)]. Rothvoss combined discrepancy-based rounding with grouping and gluing of small items to improve this to order $\log\operatorname{OPT}\log\log\operatorname{OPT}$ <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Rothvoss2013](#ref-Rothvoss2013)]. Hoberg and Rothvoss first assembled items into containers and then packed the containers into bins, obtaining order $\log\operatorname{OPT}$ <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[HobergRothvoss2017](#ref-HobergRothvoss2017)]. The latter two algorithms are randomized, and their bounds hold relative to the configuration LP. These logarithmic upper bounds are compatible with Theorem <a href="#thm:unbounded-gap" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:unbounded-gap">2</a>: the constructed instances can grow very rapidly with the prescribed gap. We do not obtain a matching logarithmic lower bound as a function of their size.

Earlier counterexamples and rounding obstructions explain two obstacles to a larger gap. Caprara, Dell’Amico, Díaz-Díaz, Iori, and Rizzi encoded edge-coloring obstructions in scalar bin sizes to produce instances without the integer round-up property <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CapraraDellAmicoDiazDiazIoriRizzi2015](#ref-CapraraDellAmicoDiazDiazIoriRizzi2015)]. Such graph encodings provide a related way to transfer a combinatorial obstruction into packing, but those examples do not establish unbounded additive gaps. Newman, Neiman, and Nikolov, and Eisenbrand, Pálvölgyi, and Rothvoss used discrepancy constructions to prove logarithmic obstructions for algorithms restricted to rounding a supplied fractional support <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[NewmanNeimanNikolov2012](#ref-NewmanNeimanNikolov2012), [EisenbrandPalvolgyiRothvoss2013](#ref-EisenbrandPalvolgyiRothvoss2013)]. An integral packing may use other feasible configurations. A gap for the full configuration LP must therefore control all such packings, rather than only the chosen support. This is the task of our extraction argument.

The restriction to at most five items per bin does not fix the number of distinct numerical sizes. Goemans and Rothvoss proved exact polynomial-time solvability for a fixed number of sizes <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GoemansRothvoss2014](#ref-GoemansRothvoss2014)], and Koana and Kumabe have announced a fixed-parameter algorithm in that parameter <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[KoanaKumabe2026](#ref-KoanaKumabe2026)]. These results concern a different parameter regime. For subset-pattern formulations, the connection to discrepancy of permutations yields bounds depending on the number of items even when bin cardinality is fixed <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[EisenbrandPalvolgyiRothvoss2013](#ref-EisenbrandPalvolgyiRothvoss2013)]; the upper bounds for set covering with ordered replacement likewise retain a logarithmic dependence on the number of items for fixed cardinality <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[EisenbrandEtAl2011](#ref-EisenbrandEtAl2011)].

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### The graph-to-packing construction

A vertex cover of a graph is a set of vertices containing at least one endpoint of every edge. We prove a quantitative reduction from this problem. For every fixed $c\ge0$ and $\rho>0$, an explicitly represented graph with $n$ vertices and a cover target $k$ produces $5B$ items. A cover of size at most $k$ gives a $B$-bin packing, while every packing into at most $B+c$ bins gives a cover of size at most $k+\rho n$. Theorem <a href="#thm:reduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:reduction">6</a> states the exact interface. The constants depend on $c$ and $\rho$; for each fixed pair, the explicit list of items and their binary encodings have polynomial length in the graph input.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### Two interval families force a choice.

For a chosen positive integer $d$, the first ingredient is a pair of finite rooted trees with a quota at each positive depth. Each tree separately has a marking that meets every root-to-leaf path at least $d$ times while respecting these quotas. When the same quotas are shared between both trees, however, the two trees cannot both have every path marked even once. Depth-constrained path hitting also occurs in the tree formulation of resource minimization for fire containment <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[ChalermsookChuzhoy2010](#ref-ChalermsookChuzhoy2010)]. We prove the particular two-tree property directly in Lemma <a href="#lem:competing-trees" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:competing-trees">5</a>.

For each graph vertex we represent the two trees by nested interval cells, with separate plus and minus regions. Cells at the same depth are well separated. A local resource of a given length can cover points in at most one cell at the corresponding depth; the number of such resources is its depth quota. If every leaf cell requires $d$ units of coverage, the competing-tree property forces almost $d$ additional, global resources onto one of the two sides. The plus-side allocation will determine the extracted vertex cover. Short test intervals for the incident edges are placed beside the plus tree.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Five item roles encode interval coverage.

An intended bin has a row item, an anchor item, a global auxiliary item, a local auxiliary item, and a flag item. Rows, anchors, and local auxiliaries carry graph-vertex labels. A row carries a baseline coordinate $b$. In one kind of bin with equal labels, the auxiliary lengths move it to a completion coordinate $h\le b$, and the anchor supplies a deadline $z$. The bin fits precisely when $h\le z$. Its half-open interval $[h,b)$ records the positions crossed by that move. Shifting $d$ deadlines from the right endpoint of a test interval to its left endpoint forces $d$ additional completions at or before every point in that interval. Comparing prefix counts turns this into a coverage requirement. The elementary identity underlying this step is illustrated in Figure <a href="#fig:interval-mechanism" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:interval-mechanism">1</a>.

<figure id="fig:interval-mechanism">

<figcaption>Interval coverage and deadline demand at one vertex label. Only representative nested tree cells are shown; vertical extent depicts containment. Leaf and job tests are left-closed and right-open. A row with completion $h$ covers $[h, b)$, while moving $d$ deadlines from a test’s right endpoint to its left endpoint adds exactly $d$ to the deadline prefix count on that test. When row and unshifted-deadline baseline multisets agree, covering every test at least $d$ times supplies the prefix inequalities needed to match completions to deadlines.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-figsrc-fig-interval-mechanism"></a>

The other kind of bin pairs a job row with an anchor called a key. Each graph edge has many repeated job positions at both endpoints. A job row is either short or long. A long row matched to a key at the same position must use either a limited resource called a permit belonging to the key’s edge or a resource belonging to an earlier edge. The numerical coordinates prevent keys from using later rows or later-edge resources. A key using an earlier row reduces the earlier rows available for deadline bins; the resulting prefix deficit forces additional coverage at the current job. When the plus allocation is small, this compensation forces many exact-position long matches. Conservation of earlier-edge resources bounds how many of those matches can avoid the current edge’s permits. If both endpoint plus allocations were small, too many long matches would need that edge’s permits. Thus every edge has a sufficiently funded endpoint.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
###### Arbitrary packings and the two consequences.

All coordinates and subclass constraints are encoded in ordinary scalar sizes near $1/5$. An arbitrary feasible packing need not consist of the intended bins. Integer scores and conservation of their total leave only a bounded number of items outside four five-item patterns. A second score orders the vertex labels; conservation over label prefixes bounds the number of unequal-label matches at each vertex. Crucially, the error at a vertex is independent of the number of positions in its construction. These bounds allow the interval and edge arguments to apply to every packing into at most $B+c$ bins.

For completeness, a vertex cover chooses one of two local states at each vertex, funding the plus side on the cover and the minus side elsewhere. The cover condition ensures that all edge resources can be assigned. For the fractional witness, instead take the four-vertex clique and give weight one half to each local state at every vertex. Each state uses every labeled item at its vertex exactly once, and the average demands equal the global inventories. An explicit distribution over physical item copies gives feasible configurations of total weight $B$. The cardinality bound of five supplies the matching dual lower bound. This fractional mixture does not require the states to coexist as an integral packing; cover extraction rules out such a packing within the prescribed allowance.

Section <a href="#sec:competing-trees" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:competing-trees">2</a> proves the competing-tree lemma. Section <a href="#sec:instance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:instance">3</a> constructs the rational item sizes and proves their pattern, label, and order properties. Section <a href="#sec:completeness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:completeness">4</a> packs the items from a vertex cover, and Section <a href="#sec:fractional" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:fractional">5</a> builds the exact fractional mixture. Section <a href="#sec:soundness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:soundness">6</a> extracts a small cover from an arbitrary packing with extra bins. Finally, Section <a href="#sec:consequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:consequences">7</a> applies this common reduction first to the unconditional gap and then, using Håstad’s satisfiability gap <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[Hastad2001](#ref-Hastad2001)], to ordinary NP-hardness.

<a id="sec:competing-trees"></a>
### Two competing trees

In a rooted tree $T$, the depth of a vertex is its distance from the root; write $V_\ell(T)$ for the set of vertices at depth $\ell$. A *marking* is a set of vertices at positive depths. It *hits* a path if the path contains a marked vertex. The following construction provides two trees that each admit many hits along every path, but cannot both be hit when their depth quotas are shared.

Depth-constrained path hitting appears in the tree formulation of resource minimization for fire containment studied by Chalermsook and Chuzhoy <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[ChalermsookChuzhoy2010](#ref-ChalermsookChuzhoy2010), Section 2]. The obstruction below uses the same disjoint-branch counting principle as their construction in Section 6: more disjoint branches than marks available in their depth range leave one branch unhit. Here the quotas vary with depth, and retaining paths according to their red or blue vertex counts produces separate multiple-hit markings but excludes a shared one-hit marking. We prove this two-tree property in full.

<a id="stmt-lem-competing-trees"></a>
<div id="lem:competing-trees" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Competing trees). *For every integer $d\ge1$, there are two finite rooted trees $T^+,T^-$, an integer $L\ge1$, and positive integer quotas $p_1,\ldots,p_L$ with the following properties.*

<a id="eq:tree-separate-marking"></a>
1.  *All leaves of both trees have depth $L$. Each tree $T^\sigma$, $\sigma\in\{+,-\}$, has a marking $\mathcal M^\sigma$ such that $$\label{eq:tree-separate-marking}
     \begin{aligned}
     |\mathcal M^\sigma\cap V_\ell(T^\sigma)|&\le p_\ell
     &&(1\le\ell\le L),\\
     |\mathcal M^\sigma\cap\pi|&\ge d
     &&\text{for every root-to-leaf path $\pi$ in $T^\sigma$}.
     \end{aligned}$$*

<a id="eq:tree-combined-quota"></a>
2.  *For any markings $\mathcal A^+$ and $\mathcal A^-$ satisfying the combined bounds $$\label{eq:tree-combined-quota}
     |\mathcal A^+\cap V_\ell(T^+)|+
     |\mathcal A^-\cap V_\ell(T^-)|\le p_\ell
     \qquad(1\le\ell\le L),$$ at least one of the two trees has a root-to-leaf path that is not hit by its marking.*

*The trees, quotas, and separate markings are constructible by a finite deterministic procedure depending only on $d$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-competing-trees"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-competing-trees</summary>

*Proof.* We first build one auxiliary tree $\mathcal T$, with some vertices colored red or blue. We then obtain $T^+$ and $T^-$ by retaining suitable complete paths in two copies of $\mathcal T$.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
###### Phases and global depths.

Begin with a single root at depth $h_0=0$, and perform $2d-1$ phases. At the start of phase $a$, let the current leaves be $u_1,\ldots,u_N$, all at depth $h_{a-1}$. The phase will add $N$ levels, so set $h_a=h_{a-1}+N$. Give the new global depths the quotas <a id="eq:tree-phase-quotas"></a>$$\label{eq:tree-phase-quotas}
 p_{h_{a-1}+i}=2^{i-1}=\frac{M_i}{2},
 \qquad M_i=2^i,\qquad 1\le i\le N.$$ The indices $1,\ldots,N$ are local to this phase. The successive depth blocks $(h_{a-1},h_a]$ are disjoint, so each positive depth receives its quota exactly once.

<a id="eq:tree-continuations"></a>
For each $i$, attach to $u_i$ exactly $M_i$ paths of $i$ edges, called *spines*. They share their starting vertex $u_i$ and are otherwise vertex-disjoint. Color half of their endpoints red and half blue. Each such endpoint has depth $h_{a-1}+i$. If $i<N$, attach to each endpoint exactly $$\label{eq:tree-continuations}
 1+\sum_{j=i+1}^{N}2^{j-1}$$ paths of $N-i$ edges, sharing only that endpoint. Call these paths the *continuation chains*. All vertices introduced in distinct spines or continuation chains are new, except for the explicitly shared starting vertices. In particular, constructions below different $u_i$ are disjoint. No newly introduced vertex on a continuation chain is colored. For $i=N$, the spine endpoint already has depth $h_a$ and no continuation is added.
